Let's derive from Intel mnemonics:
`FADD ST(0), ST(1)` means ST(0) += ST(1), no pop. Opcode D8 C1.
`FADD ST(1), ST(0)` means ST(1) += ST(0), no pop. Opcode D8 C1? Wait same? Actually Intel:
D8 C0+i: FADD ST(0), ST(i) — ST(0) = ST(0) + ST(i).
DC C0+i: FADD ST(i), ST(0) — ST(i) = ST(i) + ST(0).
Then `FADDP ST(i), ST(0)` = ST(i) += ST(0); pop ST(0); opcode DE C0+i.
A no operand `FADDP` is `FADDP ST(1), ST(0)`.
In AT&T, operand order reversed: `faddp %st, %st(1)` means source `%st`, dest `%st(1)`? Because AT&T `op src, dst`. Intel instruction `FADDP ST(1), ST(0)` has source ST(1), dest ST(0)? Intel manual says `FADDP ST(1), ST(0)` (or `FADDP ST(1), ST`). If AT&T reverses: `faddp %st, %st(1)` would correspond to Intel `FADDP ST(1), ST`: source `%st` (ST0), dest `%st(1)`, good. Therefore after product in st0 and accumulator in st1, `faddp %st, %st(1)` adds product to accumulator and pops, leaving accumulator in st0. This is exactly what we want. I think `faddp %st, %st(1)` is correct. Many inline asm examples use `faddp %%st, %%st(1)` to add st0 to st1 and pop? Let's check: If st0=1, st1=2, `faddp %st, %st(1)` yields st0=3? Let's craft template with memory inputs and careful order:
```
asm volatile (
    "movw %[y], %%si\n\t"       /* y ptr for zero loop; si clobbered */
    "movw %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "xorw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "jcxz 9f\n\t"
"1:\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, (%%si)\n\t"
    ...
    "addw $8, %%si\n\t"
    "decw %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 1b\n\t"
"9:\n\t"
    "movw %[m], %%dx\n\t"
    "testw %%dx, %%dx\n\t"
    "jz 8f\n\t"
    "movw %[A], %%bx\n\t"
    "movw %[tmp], %%di\n\t"
"2:\n\t"
    "xorw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, (%%di)\n\t"
    ...
    "movw %[x], %%si\n\t"
    "movw %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "jcxz 5f\n\t"
    "fldl (%%di)\n\t"
"3:\n\t"
    ...
    "jnz 3b\n\t"
    "fstpl (%%di)\n\t"
"5:\n\t"
    "movw %[n], %%ax\n\t"
    "addw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "addw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "addw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "subw %%ax, %%bx\n\t"
    "movw %[y], %%si\n\t"
    "movw %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "jcxz 7f\n\t"
"4:\n\t"
    ...
    "jnz 4b\n\t"
"7:\n\t"
    "addw $8, %%di\n\t"
    "decw %%dx\n\t"
    "jnz 2b\n\t"
"8:\n\t"
    :
    : [A] "m" (A), [x] "m" (x), [y] "m" (y), [tmp] "m" (tmp),
      [n] "i" (_PB_N), [m] "i" (_PB_M)
    : "ax", "bx", "cx", "dx", "si", "di", "memory"
);
```

Detailed final template with labels:
```
asm volatile (
    "movw %[y], %%si\n\t"
    "movw %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "xorw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "jcxz 0f\n\t"
"1:\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, (%%si)\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, 2(%%si)\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, 4(%%si)\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, 6(%%si)\n\t"
    "addw $8, %%si\n\t"
    "decw %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 1b\n\t"
"0:\n\t"
    "movw %[m], %%dx\n\t"
    "testw %%dx, %%dx\n\t"
    "jnz 2f\n\t"
    "jmp 8f\n\t"
"2:\n\t"
    "movw %[A], %%bx\n\t"
    "movw %[tmp], %%di\n\t"
"3:\n\t"
    "xorw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, (%%di)\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, 2(%%di)\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, 4(%%di)\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, 6(%%di)\n\t"
    "movw %[x], %%si\n\t"
    "movw %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "jcxz 5f\n\t"
    "fldl (%%di)\n\t"
"4:\n\t"
    "fldl (%%bx)\n\t"
    "fmull (%%si)\n\t"
    "faddp\n\t"
    "addw $8, %%bx\n\t"
    "addw $8, %%si\n\t"
    "decw %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 4b\n\t"
    "fstpl (%%di)\n\t"
"5:\n\t"
    "movw %[n], %%ax\n\t"
    "addw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "addw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "addw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "subw %%ax, %%bx\n\t"
    "movw %[y], %%si\n\t"
    "movw %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "jcxz 7f\n\t"
"6:\n\t"
    "fldl (%%si)\n\t"
    "fldl (%%bx)\n\t"
    "fmull (%%di)\n\t"
    "faddp\n\t"
    "fstpl (%%si)\n\t"
    "addw $8, %%bx\n\t"
    "addw $8, %%si\n\t"
    "decw %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 6b\n\t"
"7:\n\t"
    "addw $8, %%di\n\t"
    "decw %%dx\n\t"
    "jnz 3b\n\t"
"8:\n\t"
    :
    : [A] "m" (A), [x] "m" (x), [y] "m" (y), [tmp] "m" (tmp),
      [n] "i" (_PB_N), [m] "i" (_PB_M)
    : "ax", "bx", "cx", "dx", "si", "di", "memory"
);
```

Potential issue: We did not use `fwait` after y loop's last `fstpl` before ending asm. This is important: In a C asm statement, if we write separate string literals on separate lines:
```

"1:\n\t"
...
```
The C compiler concatenates the string literals into one string, preserving the `\n\t` escapes. So the asm code has newlines between instructions. A label `"1:\n\t"` is at the start of a new line after the newline from previous `\n\t`, so valid. good.

```
asm volatile (
    "movw %[y], %%si\n\t"
    "movw %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "xorw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "jcxz 0f\n\t"
"1:\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, (%%si)\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, 2(%%si)\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, 4(%%si)\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, 6(%%si)\n\t"
    "addw $8, %%si\n\t"
    "decw %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 1b\n\t"
"0:\n\t"
    "movw %[m], %%dx\n\t"
    "testw %%dx, %%dx\n\t"
    "jnz 2f\n\t"
    "jmp 8f\n\t"
"2:\n\t"
    "movw %[A], %%bx\n\t"
    "movw %[tmp], %%di\n\t"
"3:\n\t"
    "xorw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, (%%di)\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, 2(%%di)\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, 4(%%di)\n\t"
    "movw %%ax, 6(%%di)\n\t"
    "movw %[x], %%si\n\t"
    "movw %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "jcxz 5f\n\t"
"4:\n\t"
    "fldl (%%bx)\n\t"
    "fmull (%%si)\n\t"
    "faddl (%%di)\n\t"
    "fstpl (%%di)\n\t"
    "addw $8, %%bx\n\t"
    "addw $8, %%si\n\t"
    "decw %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 4b\n\t"
"5:\n\t"
    "movw %[n], %%ax\n\t"
    "addw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "addw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "addw %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "subw %%ax, %%bx\n\t"
    "movw %[y], %%si\n\t"
    "movw %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "jcxz 7f\n\t"
"6:\n\t"
    "fldl (%%bx)\n\t"
    "fmull (%%di)\n\t"
    "faddl (%%si)\n\t"
    "fstpl (%%si)\n\t"
    "addw $8, %%bx\n\t"
    "addw $8, %%si\n\t"
    "decw %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 6b\n\t"
    "fwait\n\t"
"7:\n\t"
    "addw $8, %%di\n\t"
    "decw %%dx\n\t"
    "jnz 3b\n\t"
"8:\n\t"
    :
    : [A] "m" (A), [x] "m" (x), [y] "m" (y), [tmp] "m" (tmp),
      [n] "i" (_PB_N), [m] "i" (_PB_M)
    : "ax", "bx", "cx", "dx", "si", "di", "cc", "memory"
);
```
